Investing - Theory, News &amp; General • Re: Why do so many people think inflation is bad?

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

If your grocery bill goes up 10% but so do your stocks, why would that be a problem?
Stocks don't always rise when inflation does. In 2022 inflation was 8%. What did the stock market do in 2022?

Lost -19% (max drawdown of -25%).

The market went up 25% in 2023 but then you broke even from 2022's -19% loss. You lost money in REAL terms over those two years not only because nominal return was 0% but inflation was 8% plus whatever it was in 2023 (maybe 5% if memory serves).

Long term stocks have been a good hedge against inflation but people are hurting in the shorter term.

Investing - Theory, News &amp; General • Re: Article: Do Bond Investors Get Rewarded for Taking Dur. Risk? (P. 1) Edward F. McQuarrie, William J. Bernstein, 11/6

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

1. diversification within the bond space is difficult, because almost every type is correlated near .90 with the others.
2. There is no good answer to your questions about correlations, because these are not stationary over time. However, as a general rule, Treasuries have a correlation with stocks near 0, while corporate bonds have a higher, non-zero correlation.
Regarding point 1, term risk, credit risk, and prepayment risk are sufficiently uncorrelated for bond diversification if the goal is to enhance expected return and reduce volatility of a bond portfolio. But it cones at the expense of increasing correlation with equities, so that the volatility of a stock and bond portfolio typically will be increased.

Regarding point 2, sample correlations or sample covariances are not stationary over time. That is an artifact of sample bias and general low quality of investment return samples. Actual correlation or covariance is a property of the distributions and joint distributions of future return. We don’t know what those distributions are, or if they are non-stationary, and even if they are, we don’t know how rapidly they change. There are some explicit transitional phenomena, like Fannie and Freddie dropping assumability as an allowed property of conforming loans, and the impact of that on extension risk of MBS.

Ultimately, though, what most investors care about with respect to using bonds to diversify equity risk is what the sample correlations look like during major corrections and bear markets. Even more salient is the performance of an overall portfolio during times of market stress.

Thus, when I evaluate different bond portfolios, my preference is to stress test different equity and bond portfolios using a collection of different periods of market stress— bear markets, periods of inflation acceleration, etc.

Ralph Lauren: 3 Key Takeaways for Long-Term Investors

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Pulling back from lower-tier doors trades short-term volume for brand equity, a tradeoff the article's third takeaway leaves unresolved.

On its second-quarter earnings call, held on Nov. 5, 2026, Ralph Lauren (RL -1.15%) announced that it raised full-year guidance after quarterly revenue and profit beat internal targets. The apparel maker is expanding margins by selling more goods at full price and building its luxury presence across global markets. That pricing power protects profitability even when store traffic slows in regions such as Europe. Three things management said stand out.

Asia has become the primary growth engine for Ralph Lauren, driven by direct-to-consumer (DTC) sales, which include company-owned stores and websites. Revenue in China rose by over 35% as management expanded its retail ecosystem in top cities.

Ralph Lauren increased its Average Unit Retail (AUR), the average price paid per item, by 9% during the quarter. That pricing power helped boost adjusted gross margin, the percentage of revenue kept after direct costs, to 70%.

To protect its premium positioning, Ralph Lauren is pulling back from off-price channels, which sell excess stock at deep discounts. Management is intentionally exiting lower-tier department stores to focus on higher-margin, full-price sales.

Marketing investments reached 8.6% of sales as Ralph Lauren funded major campaign activations. Brand storytelling drives customer acquisition across channels, and management intends to maintain high reinvestment levels alongside margin growth.

Pricing discipline, retail channel expansion in Asia, and direct customer engagement anchor future value as management navigates traffic headwinds in Europe.

What A Road Sign With A Black 'Y' Means (And Other Letters, Too)

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

We have covered all manner of weird road signs on Jalopnik before, including some seriously goofy ones. While oddities from around the globe are fun to explore and explain, it is a little more relevant to touch on road signs that crop up on U.S. soil. Some of these will no doubt be familiar, but others may not, so there is good reason to delve into them and provide a quick explainer. The black "Y" sign was the one that initially piqued our interest, which, by the way, highlights that a Y-shaped intersection is to be found up ahead. That led us to look at other weird letter- and symbol-focused road signs drivers may come across.

The obvious candidates are "P" for parking areas and "H" that marks hospitals, which are universally understood, but throughout this article, we will also explore what those weird "T" or upturned "U" signs mean, in addition to winding "S" and plus-symbol signs. That includes where drivers are most likely to find them and how best to react when they do. Listed in no particular order, here are our picks of eight letter- or symbol-focused road signs that we thought are worth exploring in a bit more detail.

As already explained briefly above, a sign that depicts a capitalized "Y" is there to let drivers know of a Y-shaped three-way intersection or a forked road ahead. The sign will typically be a diamond shape with a yellow background, which means it's a simple warning sign alerting drivers that the road splits. The choices of where to go are obvious — either left or right — but what might be less obvious is how the right of way is determined in a three-way intersection that isn't controlled by any other signs.

The general rule is that drivers must yield to the car on the right. Then there's always the first-to-arrive rule, which works as you would intend — the first to arrive at the split goes first. Of course, some drivers will assume right of way when it is not deserved, or perhaps they just do not see the other car(s). In such instances, it's best to yield to the other vehicle even if you believe you should have the right of way to ensure everyone gets past the intersection without incident.

When a road splits like this, it pays to be especially cautious. Drivers may attempt to dart from one lane to the next when correcting a last-minute wrong decision, plus motorbikes or cyclists could be hiding in your blind spots, so try to ensure you have sufficient space around you if possible. The NHTSA highlights that roughly one third of all traffic fatalities occur at signposted intersections, so doubling down on cautiousness here is never a bad idea, no matter how simple it may first appear.

Surprise surprise, the letter "T" on a road sign warns of a T-shaped intersection — we would hope that you managed to work that one out. The road you are traveling on is about to end and merge with another. Once again, this is a classic warning sign finished in a yellow and black colorway, on a diamond-shaped sign that lets drivers know something's coming up and they will have to act accordingly.

In this instance, drivers must yield to traffic on the intersecting road — they don't have the right of way here — before heading either left or right. There will usually be a stop or yield sign prior to the intersection itself, and to avoid any frustrating bumps with other drivers making late decisions, it's best to get into the required lane early rather than last-minute. Thankfully, of the deadliest intersections on U.S. roads, not many are T-shaped, but the same rules as above apply here. Give yourself space, keep an eye on your blind spots, and don't rush. Oh, and don't forget to use your turn signals either, even if you are in a dedicated turn lane.

Again, we won't be handing out prizes to anyone who is able to correctly guess this one. A cross or "plus" symbol indicates that a crossroad is coming up, and just like the other aforementioned sign, this too will be depicted on a black and yellow diamond-shaped warning sign. Here, traffic can and likely will be coming from all directions, so it's imperative to take the time that's needed and approach it with care. Be aware that drivers on the opposite leg might try to cross ahead of you as you pull out, and be sure to check traffic from both left and right directions. As always, make sure to follow the instructions depicted on any regulatory signs posted in your direction, as these might detail give-way instructions.

There is a good case to make here in favor of the roundabout. In many ways, it is a far better design than the traditional intersection, with fewer conflict points, no left-turns across the flow of traffic, and lower speeds. Roundabouts might seem perplexing to some, but according to data from the Federal Highway Administration, they have been proven to reduce injury and fatal crashes by 76% and 90%, respectively, plus they are far more time-efficient than traffic signals, so perhaps road users ought to just get used to them.

OK, that's not strictly true. This sign, which isn't an "S" at all — it's just a winding arrow — signifies that a winding road is up around. In other words, the natural habitat for a Miata owner who's looking for some engaging roads to make the most of their drop-top weekend car. Of course, the sign isn't actually there to tell drivers that the road is a great place to go hooning. Instead, it lets them know the road ahead has several curves one after another and that they should approach that section of road with some caution.

In fact, these "S" signs will frequently be accompanied by another advisory speed sign that suggests the maximum safe speed for the section. Drivers would be wise to pay attention to these speeds, as around 25% of all fatal road incidents happen on a curved section of road, with common causes being cars either drifting off into trees, rocks, or other fixed objects, or indeed drifting across the center of the road and causing a head-on collision with other traffic.

Anyone who can't figure this one out should probably start taking notes here. An upturned U-shaped arrow crossed out in red, of course, means that U-turns are not permitted. Drivers should continue on, take a left or right turn if available, and find another suitable route. Exceptions will not be made for those in classic red-and-white Gran Torinos.

This is the first sign featured on our list that strays from that typical yellow and black warning sign colorway. Instead, this is a rectangular regulatory sign with a white background. These signs can crop up just about anywhere, but they can commonly be spotted in business districts, on narrow streets, or busier stretches of the nation's roads, such as on multi-lane highways and freeways.

For those who may question the legality of U-turns when this sign isn't present, drivers can usually perform such a move in these circumstances: They should be in the leftmost or center-left lane, signalling their intention, and keeping an eye out for traffic as per any other manoeuvre.

A sideways "T" sign indicates that a side road is coming up. Depending on which way the T is oriented, the sign highlights whether the side road is on the right or the left. If the sign instead looks like an F or an E, but with an extended top or bottom, this means that two side roads are coming up on the right-hand side.

Specifically, this sign will appear when the side roads on the right are particularly close to one another. The hazard this poses to drivers on the major road is quite obvious; they should be wary of traffic entering from the right, and should be prepared to slow down. The sign itself is a typical diamond-shaped yellow-and-black warning sign, which does not indicate right of way. Typically, those on the major road will have the right of way, while those on the indicated side roads will have to yield to traffic on the major road.

The sign for a roundabout shows three arrows forming a circle, and once again, it will be a yellow-and-black diamond-shaped warning sign. Roundabouts always run in a counter-clockwise direction in North America. They can have numerous lanes and are an alternative to crossroads. Here, the right of way rules are simple and uniform: Drivers must always give way to traffic on the left — in other words, the cars that are already on the roundabout.

Getting the right lane is crucial for avoiding accidents; the leftmost lane is used for turning left, whereas the rightmost lanes are used for turning right, and both can be used for going straight on. These are the rules on multi-lane roundabouts unless signposted otherwise — this way, traffic in either lane does not cross unnecessarily.

As for whether drivers need to use turn signals on a roundabout or not, the answer is yes. It's never a bad idea to let other drivers know where you are going; even if you are comfortable in your own abilities to get across safely, it's best practice to help other motorists out by indicating to them your next intended move.

Canada Goose: 3 Key Takeaways for Long-Term Investors

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Store traffic is the open question: the retail network is where the apparel gains either hold up or stall.

On its second quarter of fiscal 2027 earnings call, held on Nov. 5, 2026, Canada Goose (GOOS +2.17%) announced a 5% revenue increase to 274 million Canadian dollars while expanding its gross profit margins. Long-term investors need to evaluate whether the luxury outerwear maker can build a sustainable year-round business while addressing headwinds in retail store traffic. Three things management said stand out.

Management expanded gross margins through improved sourcing, manufacturing efficiency, and higher pricing, rather than relying on short-term external boosts. These operational upgrades helped expand gross margins even as retail network costs increased.

Expanding beyond traditional heavy parkas creates a balanced business model that reduces reliance on cold weather. Sales growth in lighter apparel and rainwear indicates that retail customers increasingly purchase these products from the brand year-round.

Lower physical store traffic dragged down direct-to-consumer (DTC) comparable sales, which measure performance at locations open for over a year. Better online sales and store conversion rates partially cushioned the drop, but store foot traffic remains a clear challenge.

Wholesale orders are expanding, and management projects steady progress toward its annual profitability targets. Operational upgrades and pricing discipline support long-term margins while leadership tracks store traffic recovery.

Execution now centers on boosting store traffic and improving retail conversion during the peak winter buying season.

Use Rocket Money if you have more subscriptions and provider bills than you’ll cancel or negotiate yourself, since its paid plans do that work for you. With only a few bills, skip the monthly fee and make the cancellations and calls on your own.

Rocket Money’s free plan finds your subscriptions, but having it cancel them takes Premium, typically $7 to $14 a month at a price you pick, and having it lower your bills takes Premium+ at $15 a month plus tax. A household with only a few bills saves the fee by making its own calls and cancellations, and anyone uneasy about Rocket Mortgage and the other Rocket companies receiving their transaction data should look at other budgeting apps.

Where was Nvidia’s security team?

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Outsiders had little reason to notice when Nvidia’s longtime chief security officer and head of product security stepped down in August. Just two and a half months later, CEO Jensen Huang himself left the company over growing questions about his extramarital relationships — starting with an executive at Megaspeed, a Singapore data center company that U.S. investigators suspect of funneling Nvidia chips to China, then widening to reports about two separate affairs with Nvidia employees.

At the time, David Reber, who spent four years as CSO and seven at Nvidia overall, said he was leaving for health reasons. “This wasn’t a pivot I had planned. But I know what needs to come first: family and health,” he wrote on LinkedIn in late August, disclosing that he’d been diagnosed with young-onset Parkinson’s more than two years ago.

Reber’s replacement, Saša Zdjelar, previously spent 17 years at ExxonMobil in Houston, leaving as a senior IT manager before spending one year at Salesforce and several years at the cybersecurity company ReversingLabs. Zdjelar didn’t respond to questions Thursday about the transition or what the security team knew.

Nothing public connects Reber’s or Zdjelar’s role changes to the Megaspeed matter, but it seems fair to wonder what Nvidia’s security team knew about the government’s scrutiny of Megaspeed and about Huang’s relationships, and whether anyone raised concerns. Reber didn’t respond to questions Thursday. Nvidia also has not answered our related questions.

Not all of those relationships are the business of the security team. Relationships with subordinates typically fall to HR, legal, and the board. But an affair with an executive at a company under federal investigation is a different animal. (Megaspeed has denied any wrongdoing.) Security specialists say affairs can create vulnerabilities that foreign intelligence services look to exploit.

Catching that kind of exposure isn’t easy. The way intelligence services collect information, much of it through everyday conversation rather than hacking, makes it especially difficult for security teams to identify risks, according to retired senior intelligence officer Nicholas Eftimiades, who says he has trained more than 1,000 corporate security professionals.

Some people inside Nvidia clearly knew parts of the picture. Nvidia’s top lawyer was engaged with the government’s Megaspeed inquiry more than a year ago, The New York Times reported earlier this week. (Nvidia has said it regularly contacts officials.) Reports about Huang’s relationships with employees suggest colleagues also knew of them, even if the behavior is not expressly forbidden by Nvidia’s code of conduct. The Times cited five Nvidia employees who said he was involved with a vice president who reported directly to him, and Reuters, citing two people, later reported a second relationship. What’s unclear is whether any of that reached security.

Even if it had reached Nvidia’s security team, Eftimiades, who wrote Chinese Espionage Operations and Tactics in 2020, said a security team’s resources are often limited. For one thing, they’re often underfunded because, unlike some other cost centers, they don’t drive revenue. It’s astonishingly common for CEOs to answer a security team’s warnings with “not a nickel more than compliance,” he said.

Many companies also define security very narrowly, specialists say. In an opinion piece in The Hill, Don Aviv, CEO of the corporate investigations firm Interfor International, and author Jeremy Hurewitz, wrote, “Too many companies still approach security primarily as a matter of protecting facilities, preventing cyberattacks, and responding to physical threats.” The two argued that “[s]ecurity teams need to pay greater attention to insider threats, including the cultivation of executives and employees by foreign intelligence services, suspicious business relationships, conflicts of interest and attempts to obtain sensitive information through trusted personal contacts.”

Still, some of it is beyond what any team can do. Speaking generally, not about Huang, Eftimiades offered a hypothetical in which a CEO complains that he can’t do something because “I’m fighting a board who doesn’t want to do this.” That tells an intelligence asset who the obstacle is. The service then asks, “Who’s the sticking point here?” and looks for ways into that person’s email and phone.

What a rival intelligence service wants is often less technology than policy insight, including who’s pushing to change export rules and who the biggest blocker is. “Intel world tends to be like cancer,” he said. “Once it gets into the body, it looks to spread and looks to reach out.”

The playbook isn’t new, Eftimiades added, “particularly for China.” Any executive dealing with the country “will tell you that they’ve been approached.” He said there are also “tons of diplomat stories” of U.S. diplomats walking into a hotel room to find a woman on the bed. He said what’s newer compared with Soviet era spying is the relationships these services cultivate now often last years, not weeks.

Technology can augment that human intelligence, Eftimiades said, pointing to IMSI catchers are one example. These devices mimic a cell tower so that nearby phones connect to them. A basic version can cost about $1,000 and fit in a suitcase, he said, though government-grade models cost far more.

Eftimiades says no one knows yet if these were used against Huang, whose phone was presumably hardened such that compromises could be detected, if not stopped altogether. But either way, what Nvidia’s security team knew, and whether it could have acted on it, should become clearer as the board’s independent investigation and federal inquiries proceed.

In the meantime, Eftimiades said he hopes the whole episode is “a wake-up call” for U.S. industry, which he said is mostly “clueless” about China’s tactics. “We have lost trillions [of dollars] in intellectual property,” is his guess. “Whatever we think we’re doing isn’t working.”

Bilt Will Stop Awarding You 4% Bilt Cash Earning At Card Renewal—Unless You Change This App Setting

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

I receive compensation for content and many links on this blog. Be aware that websites may earn compensation when a customer clicks on a link, when an application is approved, or when an account is opened. Citibank is an advertising partner of this site, as is American Express, Chase, Capital One, and others. Any opinions expressed in this post are my own, and have not been reviewed, approved, or endorsed by my advertising partners. I do not write about all offers that are available -- instead focusing on miles, points, and cash back (and currencies that can be converted into the same). Terms apply to the offers and benefits listed on this page.

Bilt Rewards is changing how rewards are earned on their credit cards. Today, cardmembers have a choice:

Earn 4% back in Bilt Cash, in addition to points. That Bilt Cash can be redeemed for points-earning on housing payments, faster points-earning (accelerators) and redemptions on hotel stays or with partners like Blade, Lyft, Walgreens, etc.

Skip Bilt Cash and earn ‘up to 1.25x on housing payments’ based on your month’s spending. Bilt is moving everyone over to the second option, however existing cardmembers and those who apply, are approved and accept their card through December 31 can opt in to preserving #1. It’s possible to do better with earning up to 1.25x on housing payments, but for most people doing significant spend on the card (where they’re earning more Bilt Cash than their housing payment would consume) they’ll come out ahead earning Bilt Cash. And of course you can always choose to move over to the new model later if you prefer! You need to proactively choose continuing to earn Bilt Cash, to avoid moving over. Once you actually move over (starting at your card renewal date) it cannot be reversed. I’ve already protected my Bilt Palladium Card (See rates and fees) with the legacy earning structure. This is the card I use for most of my spending. Here’s where you go in the app to do it. Go to your wallet and select your Bilt card. Then either go to your current choice for Bonus Rewards (e.g. “Flexible Bilt Cash”) underneath Your card rewards or go to Manage card and select bonus rewards which is the last option at the bottom. Select ‘your rewards are updating’ which is the top option on the screen. The default, which is already selected, is the new rewards model. Swipe right… Select ‘flexible Bilt Cash’ and hit save. You have until your card renewal date to make this choice, but if you preserve the old earning model you can always make the (irreversible) switch later. I’ve already made my choice. And if you spend it all on accelerators, you can’t buy up for rent day anymore.

And if you’re high housing payment, you’re not necessarily going to be high spend… so the housing only option isn’t going to be great.

This math just doesn’t math very well for most people with the 2027 rules. Which means that they’re not going to get lots of new card acquisitions in 2027. Which means that the 2026 4% grandfathered rules are going to be phased out as well – if it lasts until 2028 that would be something.

And why on earth would anyone decide to get a Bilt card this November or December just to be grandfathered in to the 4%? How could anyone possibly trust these folks? Third program change in 2026 – it’s complicated, it keeps changing… yikes.

I’m sure folks including @Gary got real value out of Bilt in 2026 if they were willing to deal with them, but… 2027 prospects look bleak, no?

I’m actually pleased with the changes, and glad they allow an opt out (to keep earning the 4%, especially now that the expiration is a rolling 12-months, which makes way more sense.) All of this is hilarious because I’ve been so freaking hard on these guys, all year, and now, I’m kinda liking it, to be honest. (@L3, where you at, son?)

XRP to $1.3? Kalshi Traders Make Weak November Bets

📅 2026-11-15  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

XRP has continued to fall to levels not seen in the last three months as prolonged market volatility continues to intensify, causing the asset to post bigger daily price losses.

While momentum appears to be fading rapidly, the slow price movements have caused expectations for a November recovery to weaken, especially among prediction market traders.

Latest data from leading prediction market Kalshi shows that XRP traders have little confidence that the asset can still reclaim $1.30 before the end of the month.

While the asset saw higher expectations from Kalshi traders last month, it appears that its recent price dip below $1 has lowered their expectations as traders are assigning low probabilities to XRP reaching higher price targets this month.

Following their predictions, XRP currently has 7% odds of trading above $1.40, while the probability of reaching $1.50 stands at 8%.

More notably, bets on XRP surging to around $1.60 are even weaker, with only a 4% implied probability, suggesting that traders have increasingly lost confidence in XRP’s potential to achieve a major recovery before this month runs out.

The weak XRP predictions from Kalshi traders have come as XRP struggles to maintain momentum around its recent price levels, falling substantially below its local highs.

As of the time of writing, XRP is trading around $0.9892, representing a substantial decline of about 3.94% over the past 24 hours and an 11.3% drop over the last week.

It is important to note that the Kalshi data only reflects traders' positioning amid current market conditions and therefore does not guarantee XRP’s precise price movement in the future.

While XRP has continued to struggle below the major $1 level, it would need to stage a major recovery to reclaim the $1.30 level.

Cowboys vs 49ers: 2 players to buy, 2 to sell against San Francisco

📅 Nov 15, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

The Dallas Cowboys are coming off a narrow win over the Indianapolis Colts, and now the 7-1 San Francisco 49ers are coming to town, bringing one of the league's most complete rosters and a defense that has consistently given Dallas problems in their recent matchups.

There are some opportunities for the Cowboys, particularly with CeeDee Lamb playing at a high level, but there are also some difficult matchups that could expose weaknesses on Dallas' roster. Here are two players to buy and two to sell ahead of Sunday's game.

I mean, who else would this list start with? Lamb has been the best player for the Cowboys all season, and there's no reason to think that Dallas will stop feeding him the ball. Lamb is coming off a monster performance against Indianapolis, hauling in 10 catches for 195 yards and a touchdown on 16 targets. It was his second-highest receiving total in a game during his career.

Through nine games, Lamb has 70 receptions for 904 yards and seven touchdowns. He's already surpassed the 900-yard mark, and his 108 targets lead the Cowboys by a wide margin. The matchup against San Francisco is difficult, but Lamb is the kind of receiver who can change a game regardless of the opponent. With George Pickens possibly missing another week and Dallas dealing with inconsistencies elsewhere the offense, Dak Prescott should continue looking Lamb's way early and often.

The 49ers will undoubtedly have a plan for him, but Lamb's recent production makes him difficult to leave out of the buyers market. He's been the Cowboys' most reliable playmaker overall, and Dallas needs to find ways to get him involved.

At some point, the Cowboys have to figure out how to beat San Francisco, and Dak Prescott has to be a big part of that solution. Unfortunately, his history against the 49ers doesn't inspire much confidence. Prescott is 2-4 against San Francisco in his career, including an 0-2 record in the playoffs. In those two postseason games, he has thrown for 460 yards, two touchdowns and three interceptions. His most recent regular-season meetings haven't been much better. He threw three interceptions in the 2023 blowout loss and two more in the 2024 matchup.

Across his four regular-season games against San Francisco, Prescott has completed 65.5% of his passes for 875 yards, eight touchdowns and five interceptions. That's not necessarily disastrous production, but the turnovers have been costly, particularly against a team that knows how to capitalize on mistakes.

The 49ers have won four straight meetings against Dallas, and Prescott will have to overcome both that team history and his own struggles against this opponent. San Francisco's defense is disciplined, physical and capable of making quarterbacks pay for forcing throws.

With Fred Warner leading the unit, Prescott will have to be sharp with his reads and decision-making, especially against the blitz, where he has looked terrible this year. Dallas needs a big performance from its quarterback to pull off the upset. Given the history, though, this is a matchup where I'm selling Prescott until he proves otherwise.

With Jake Ferguson ruled out due to a knee injury, Brevyn Spann-Ford has an opportunity to take on an even bigger role in the Cowboys' offense. It might not be the most obvious matchup to exploit, but he could be a sneaky contributor against San Francisco.

Spann-Ford has caught 15 passes for 130 yards this season, and his role has been growing. Against Indianapolis, he played 55 offensive snaps compared to Ferguson's 27, catching two of three targets for 20 yards. That workload suggests the Cowboys are already comfortable giving him significant playing time.

The 49ers have plenty of defensive talent to account for, and their attention will naturally gravitate toward Lamb and the Cowboys' other primary playmakers. That could leave opportunities for Spann-Ford underneath or in the middle of the field, particularly if Dallas uses him to help with protection before releasing him into a route.

This isn't a prediction that Spann-Ford is suddenly going to become the focal point of the passing game. Rather, it's a bet that his increased playing time and the attention paid to Dallas' other weapons could create a few favorable opportunities. With Ferguson unavailable, the Cowboys need someone else to contribute from the tight end position. Spann-Ford is in a good position to do that.

The Cowboys have indicated that Jaishawn Barham could see an increased workload as injuries continue to pile up on the defensive side of the ball. That's understandable from a personnel standpoint, but it also creates a potential problem against an offense as versatile as San Francisco's.

Barham had some encouraging moments early in the season, but his limitations have become more apparent as the competition has increased. His playing time has also been limited recently, which makes the prospect of a larger role against the 49ers particularly concerning.

San Francisco is exactly the type of opponent that can put a young linebacker in difficult situations. Kyle Shanahan's offense uses motion, misdirection and mismatches to create space, and open up running lanes.

If Dallas gives him more responsibility, San Francisco will have plenty of opportunities to test him. The Cowboys need all the help they can get defensively, but this is a tough week to ask an inconsistent young player to take on a bigger role.

Danny Boyle Says Tabloid Headlines Censored In British Version Of ‘Ink’ Will Be Shown In U.S.

📅 Nov 14, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Ink director Danny Boyle on Saturday confirmed that redacted Sun newspaper headlines shown as part of an epilogue in his Netflix drama about the founding of the UK tabloid will be shown in full in the U.S. theatrical and streaming version of the film.

Speaking while receiving the Deadline Legend Awards during Deadline’s Contenders Film Los Angeles awards-season event Saturday, Boyle said an edited version showing the headlines is currently being edited into a new cut of the film in time for its December 11 release in select theaters.

The pic, based on James Graham’s play and adapted screenplay that tells the story of Rupert Murdoch and editor Larry Lamb’s takeover of the British tabloid The Sun in the late 1960s, setting the tone for new kind of journalism in the process, was unable to show the headlines owing to Britain’s stringent libel laws.

“We’ve had a battle to get the film to where it is now, and I can tell you — I can say this, can’t I? — I think that we just won the ability [to show the headlines] because we’re in America, and you have better protection for freedom of speech than we have in Britain,” Boyle said during the Q&A session at the DGA Theater, adding that he hopes the uncensored version “will be [in] the finished version of the film, which we’ve never been able to show in Britain.” Watch on Deadline

Boyle pointed to one specific headline in the epilogue that was redacted in UK versions of the pic, above an article about the 1989 death of 97 fans crushed while trying to enter Hillsborough Stadium to watch a Liverpool-Nottingham Forest FA Cup soccer game. The tabloid report blamed Liverpool fans for the tragedy, citing what turned out to fabricated police and official reports.

“There are various headlines … at the end of [Ink], and there’s one particular, which although it won’t mean anything to you, to anybody from Britain it does mean a great deal, which is that The Sun ran a terrible headline called ‘The Truth,’ which is one of the principal concerns of James Graham’s screenplay throughout the whole film,” Boyle said.“[The Sun] ran this headline, and it was a bunch of lies about the way that Liverpool fans were killed at the Hillsborough disaster,” he continued. “It’s one of the low points of British journalism, and we wanted that to be always in the film, and we weren’t able to have it in the British version. We have these redacted versions, but know that we’re in America. We’ve got it in there, and I hope that the tidal wave will then reach back to Britain, and we’ll be allowed to put them in there because it’s an important moment for freedom of speech, which you have enshrined.”

He added, “You know, it’s it’s it’s interesting that a tabloid that you know that lets it all hang out would try to stop you from doing something similar.”

The epilogue in the pic, which stars Jack O’Connell as Lamb and Guy Pearce as Murdoch, is also key as it ties The Sun’s rise to modern tabloids — and Murdoch’s eventual founding of Fox News.

“What they ignited … and the pact that they came up with to start The Sun culminates financially and principally in the birth of Fox News,” Boyle said. “Because the money that The Sun made allowed Murdoch to buy 20th Century Fox and then to finance the establishment, the extraordinary establishment, of a new terrestrial channel in America, which takes a lot of money. So there’s an umbilical cord, I think, that connects these two guys and their meeting in 1969 with where we are now.”

Ink, which has been hitting festival since its world premiere to open this year’s Venice Film Festival, is due to open in the UK on January 8, when it becomes available to stream on Netflix.

Liverpool transfer news: 'Significant' interest in £29m centre-back as Andoni Iraola ponders future without Virgil van Dijk

📅 Nov 14, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

By Lewis Nolan | 14 Nov 2026 23:43

Liverpool are keen on Lens centre-back Pierre Ismaelo Ganiou despite interest from a number of other clubs, the latest report has claimed.

The Premier League is now on pause for the November international break, and that has given Reds supporters the chance to assess the squad.

Andoni Iraola has made a positive start to life at Anfield, with the team's defence having notably improved, led by the likes of Virgil van Dijk and Ronald Araujo.

However, Van Djik has entered the final year of his contract, while Araujo is only on loan from Barcelona and could return to the Spanish side at the end of the season.

Amid uncertainty regarding the future of their defence, Football Insider report that the Reds are one of several clubs keen on signing Lens defender Ganiou, who is valued by Transfermarkt at just over £25m.

Ganiou does not have an extensive history of top-flight football considering he has played just 28 games in Ligue 1 in his entire career, though he has been impressive for Lens.

In five league matches for the French side this season, the 21-year-old has won 69% of his duels, including 73% of his ground duels.

Liverpool have typically looked to sign aerially dominant defenders, so while the Frenchman boasts an impressive 64% duel success rate in the air, he only averages 1.8 aerial duels won per game.

Van Dijk has won an average of 3.8 duels in the air in the league so far, while Jeremy Jacquet has won 2.6 aerial duels in the Premier League, and the Reds would almost certainly aim to get Ganiou closer to those figures.

While Van Dijk is 35 and is into the final year of his contract, he has shown no signs of physical decline, and he has expressed a desire to remain at Anfield.

If he was to extend his stay on Merseyside, the Reds may not need to delve into the market for another centre-back.

Joe Gomez is expected to leave, and he could be replaced in the squad by Araujo, which would then leave the club in need of a right-back.

Jacquet and Giovanni Leoni will be part of the squad for the coming seasons, while many believe that Ifeanyi Ndukwe could play a role for the team once his loan at Levante ends.

6 best and worst fantasy football matchups for Week 10

📅 Nov 14, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

The fantasy football playoffs start in just over a month, making each remaining week critical for success.

This week, we once again have four bye weeks to worry about (Bears, Broncos, Buccaneers, Eagles), as well.

Every week, I'll continue to identify and write up players that are not obviously starts or sits. Regardless of matchup, you're starting someone like Christian McCaffrey every week, so I try to go a few layers deeper than your early-round fantasy studs.

Note: Projections refer to player props in the betting and prediction markets, which are a valuable reference tool for anyone playing fantasy football.

Through three games, Shough was the QB3 overall with 24 fantasy points per game. Since, Shough has given you just 16 fantasy points per game, good for QB17 during that stretch.

The past three games were particularly tough for Shough managers, as he faced the Steelers and Browns, with a bye week jammed between.

The good news? It's time to insert Shough back into your fantasy lineup with confidence.

Week 3: Denver Broncos cheerleaders perform during the "Sunday Night Football" game against the Los Angeles Rams at Empower Field at Mile High.

Week 3: The Maracanã Stadium gets set to host the International Series game between the Baltimore Ravens and Dallas Cowboys in Rio de Janeiro, Brazil.

Week 1: Kim Thayil of Soundgarden and Mike McCready of Pearl Jam perform during halftime of the 2026 NFL Kickoff Game between the Seattle Seahawks and New England Patriots at Lumen Field.

Week 3: Denver Broncos cheerleaders perform during the "Sunday Night Football" game against the Los Angeles Rams at Empower Field at Mile High.

Week 3: The Maracanã Stadium gets set to host the International Series game between the Baltimore Ravens and Dallas Cowboys in Rio de Janeiro, Brazil.

This matchup with Carolina has a hefty 48.5-point game total, and the Saints are slight 2.5-point favorites. That suggests a tight game with shootout potential.

Shough's pass yards projection here is 251.5 yards, while multiple passing touchdowns is currently juiced in betting markets, signaling that Vegas expects a strong passing performance overall. For context, Jordan Love's pass yards/pass touchdowns combination is worse than Shough's this week.

Each week, I stress that we should opponent-adjust when doing matchups analysis, and the Panthers are the perfect example of this. Your fantasy app likely shows Shough as having a below average matchup this week, though Carolina is actually one of the more favorable matchups once we opponent-adjust.

Four touchdowns to Caleb Williams in Week 1

20 fantasy points to Deshaun Watson in Week 3

More than 400 passing yards to Jared Goff in Week 4

288 passing yards and three touchdowns to Jordan Love in Week 8

237 yards and two touchdowns to the struggling Bo Nix in Week 9

There's really just three outlier games that make Carolina's total fantasy points allowed to quarterbacks a respectable number:

The Falcons duo of Cooper Rush and Jack Strand combined for negative fantasy points in Week 2

Tanner McKee struggled against Carolina in his first game of 2026

The Buccaneers ran for over 250 yards in Week 7, so Baker Mayfield only threw for 64 yards

In short, fire up Shough as a top-eight fantasy option against the Panthers this week.

Seattle draws the second-best rushing matchup of Week 10 against Las Vegas, a defense allowing a tasty 5.0 yards per carry to opposing backs. It's also the first start of Fernando Mendoza's career, so there's an elevated chance of a couple turnovers that result in great field position for the Seahawks.

The Seahawks are touchdown favorites, so game script should be favorable for the Seahawks running back duo. Looking at Charbonnet and Jadarian Price, the veteran has quickly taken over this backfield.

In Week 9, Charbonnet carried 16 times for 77 yards, while the rookie Price had just 24 yards on nine carries. While Price did have a slick four catches for 54 receiving yards in that game, Charbonnet actually ran almost twice as many routes, so we should expect bigger receiving games from the veteran moving forward.

Turning to the betting markets, Charbonnet is nearly 50-50 to score in this contest, and his total yards prop is quite high at 76.5 yards.

Consider Charbonnet a top-15 fantasy back in Week 10, as he should flirt with 20 touches against one of the more beatable run defenses in Las Vegas.

The second-year receiver draws the best outside matchup of Week 10 against the Saints.

Cornerbacks Kool-Aid McKinstry and Martin Emerson have been getting cooked all season, and they most recently gave up over 200 yards on the outside to a considerably worse Browns passing attack.

The Saints pass rush has not gotten to the quarterback this year, as they are bottom-10 in pressure rate. Bryce Young, when given time, has been one of the NFL's best passers in 2026. When pressured, he turns into one of the worst.

McMillan already has three games this year with at least 10 targets, and he has a very strong chance to make that four games after this week. He's a fringe top-12 fantasy option at wide receiver.

You already know to start tight ends against the Lions every week, right? Good. I don't want to detail that matchup every article.

Anyway, Pitts has the third-best matchup this week against a Kansas City defense that has struggled to contain passes over the middle of the field. Pitts isn't always used like a classic tight end, which is why it's worth mentioning that the Chiefs have been vulnerable to slot receivers this season.

The game total here is reasonably high at 46.5 points, and the Falcons are only three-point underdogs. This could be a sneaky shootout game environment.

While it's been back-to-back quiet games for Pitts, the athletic tight end has still earned at least five targets in each of the past five games.

Think of Pitts as a back-end fantasy TE1 this week with serious upside given the matchup.

Pollard has been on a tear, with at least 135 total yards and multiple touchdowns in Weeks 7 and 8, prior to the team's Week 9 bye. Unfortunately, Pollard should remain parked on your fantasy bench in Week 10 against Jacksonville.

The Jaguars were already the best run defense in the NFL, and then they added DT Jordan Davis from the Eagles. The 6-foot-6, 336-pounder is one of the NFL's best run stoppers, and it's borderline unfair to pair with him Travon Walker, Josh Hines-Allen, and Arik Armstead.

The Titans are more than 10-point underdogs, and while the Titans are well-rested after their bye, the Jaguars are coming off a long break thanks to a cake walk on Thursday night against Joe Fagnano's Ravens.

I'd rather start the aforementioned Charbonnet or Woody Marks against a surprisingly beatable Browns run defense. Consensus rankings generally have Pollard higher than those two, and I'm here to tell you the public is wrong this week.

Jacoby Brissett is now on the Ravens and Gardner Minshew is undergoing season-ending elbow surgery. Round three rookie Carson Beck is suddenly thrust into NFL action against a tough Rams pass defense. And, star receiver Michael Wilson is also now out for the season due to a neck fracture.

As a result, the whole Cardinals offense should suffer, and their Vegas team total of 15 points is historically low. Allgeier's touchdown odds of 33% are hilariously low for a starting back projected for around 20 touches, and his total yards prop is just 56.5 yards.

For context, Steelers backup Rico Dowdle is projected for 49.5 total yards and a 36% chance of a touchdown. And I think we'd all agree that Dowdle is off the fantasy radar in Week 10.

Last week was the first time the Rams had a healthy Aaron Donald and Myles Garrett. The Commanders backs mustered just 44 yards on 15 carries, and overall Jayden Daniels and crew managed just 13 points.

Allgeier should be inefficient on his touches and probably won't score. He is outside the top-24 fantasy backs in Week 10 as a result.

Rinnai’s new heat pump water heaters get smarter – and easier to install

📅 Nov 14, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Rinnai has launched a new lineup of electric heat pump water heaters that adds wifi controls and more installation options to an appliance that can cut the energy needed for hot showers.

The REHPW series comes in 50-, 65-, and 80-gallon sizes. It builds on Rinnai’s existing REHP lineup, with changes designed to make the units easier to install, monitor, and maintain.

Heat pump water heaters pull heat from the surrounding air and transfer it into the tank. Moving that heat requires less electricity than producing it with conventional electric resistance elements. There’s no fuel combustion at the appliance, although emissions from its electricity supply depend on the grid.

Rinnai lists a Uniform Energy Factor, or UEF, of 3.75 for the 50-gallon model and 4.0 for the two larger versions. Higher numbers mean greater efficiency under standardized testing; actual bills depend on electricity rates, hot water use, and installation conditions.

The lineup delivers up to 91 gallons of hot water in the first hour, depending on tank size. That rating includes both stored hot water and what the unit can heat during that hour – useful context when several people take consecutive showers.

There are five operating modes, 4.5 kW backup heating elements, and a variable-speed fan designed to keep noise down. The heat pump can operate with ambient air as low as 37°F.

Installation is where some of the more practical upgrades show up.

The new Rinnai heat pump water heater units have water connections on both the top and side, giving installers more options for connecting existing plumbing. Both the air intake and exhaust are on top, allowing zero side clearance where Rinnai’s installation requirements permit.

An optional ducting kit adds flexibility for tighter spaces. Carrying handles help with moving the tank into place, and the air filter slides out horizontally from the side for cleaning.

Rinnai has also added a third tank temperature sensor to the 50- and 80-gallon models to help manage hot water delivery.

Wifi adds remote monitoring and control. Rinnai’s updated Central app supports the REHPW series, including scheduling and tools to use time-of-use electricity rates to manage energy use.

The units also carry CTA-2045 AC Level 2 certification for demand response. That gives compatible utility programs a way to communicate with the water heater and adjust its electricity use. Rinnai says the certification can help qualify homeowners for local and state utility demand-response rebates; availability depends on the program.

The series is Energy Star certified, meets NEEA Tier 4 requirements, and complies with California’s Title 24 requirements, including JA13, according to Rinnai. Warranty coverage includes 10 years for the tank and other parts, plus one year for labor.

Rinnai directs buyers to an installer for pricing, so the upfront cost – and how much a local rebate might offset it – will require a quote. However, the REHP series price on Amazon hovers around the $2,000 mark.

Painter Sascha Braunig Pushes Through the Void

📅 Nov 14, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Sascha Braunig’s paintings have a tendency to produce visceral reactions: a pit in the stomach, an eyebrow raise, a need to move closer. This is not unintended.

“I want the viewer to have a bodily experience,” Braunig says.

The paintings are bright, smooth, candy-colored—lighthearted contrasts to the psychologically intense shapes: luminously rendered heads and torsos often bound by wiry tentacles. At times the figures recede into their matching optic backgrounds, as if hiding in plain sight.

Braunig, who is 43 and splits her time between New York and Maine, has been painting with this funky verve for close to two decades. Not long after she got her MFA from Yale’s painting program in 2008, she was having solo exhibitions of her small paintings of heads at the now-defunct Foxy Production in Manhattan. Inclusions at the New Museum Triennial and MoMA PS1 soon followed. (Attention from critics came quickly as well; one called her an “inspired reanimator of Surrealism,” another a “phenom.”)

Braunig is excellent at capturing the inherent tension of living in a body—especially one that is subjected to societal expectations of femininity. She’s playing with the ways women have been contorted and cinched and squeezed for time immemorial, asked to fit into archetypes that seem at turns glamorous or sinister. Often they are both.

“Our aesthetic, our personhood is formed in relation to these cultural forces and imagery,” she says. “You can’t separate yourself out from what you’ve seen throughout your whole life.”

These themes of gender performance and corporeal control permeate “Wrist Painter,” Braunig’s most extensive exhibition to date, which opens November 13 at the Portland Art Museum. The show brings together some 45 works, including paintings, sculptures, works on paper, and sketchbooks. It will be her first solo institutional show in Maine. (A special occasion, as almost all of the pieces in the show were made a block and a half away, in her studio at the Portland arts nonprofit SPACE, over the last 15 years.)

“Sascha really stands out here in this arts ecosystem. She’s doing something really singular,” says Sayantan Mukhopadhyay, a curator at PMA who organized the show. When people think of Maine painters, they usually think of landscapes. But Braunig leans toward the cerebral, with a bit more bite. “The paradoxes are what really excite me about the work,” Mukhopadhyay says.

And the dichotomies are many. “Sascha has a way of presenting an abstraction that can read both soft and strong, feminine and violent,” says Olivia Smith, whose New York City gallery, Magenta Plains, now represents Braunig. “I think that’s what I’ve always loved: that she offers a way for the viewer to re-associate, which ends up telling them more about themselves than about Sascha.”

One of the earliest artworks in “Wrist Painter” is Sequins, from 2010. It’s from Braunig’s head series, which she began in grad school. Her process starts with a drawing, then she brings her sketch to life by making an embellished three-dimensional model. Then she’ll light the model and paint what she observes. She applies her lustrous oil paints with such precision that these strange bodily shapes start to look “real.”

She continued this system as she moved on to her bodies series a few years later, using clay resin, wire, and paper to make her maquettes. This haptic process is why works like Warm Leatherette, a 2015 painting at PMA that looks like a snatched Michelin Man encased in chartreuse padding, seem so tactile. (She has often described her choice of colors as “seasonal” or “dietary”—she finds a palette that works for one show and then cycles to a new one for the next.)

Her maquette process also lends a certain theatricality. She is literally setting up a scene, as if the entire idea of femininity were on a velvet-curtained stage, the whole specter aglow under klieg lights. What is gender if not a performance?

Braunig, who grew up in British Columbia, Canada, liked drawing and painting from a young age. Her father (a dentist) and mother (a nurse) enjoyed creative pursuits outside of work, and the house was full of art books. She loved watching Pee-wee’s Playhouse, which she credits as being just as formative, even more so, than going to museums.

Her parents encouraged her to attend an intensive summer art program at Skidmore College in upstate New York when she was 16. It was there that she first learned about Cooper Union, the New York City school where artists Lee Krasner and Wangechi Mutu got their start. Braunig decided it was the place for her.

New York City was the perfect place for the budding artist. “I was totally in love. It maybe ruined me forever,” she jokes now. She went to Yale right after Cooper, and then she moved to Maine in 2010, in her mid-20s. “I was really fortunate to have a New York gallery as I left the city, because I was able to have shows and have my work represented here even though I wasn’t here.”

Living somewhere far less expensive was also a game changer—she didn’t need to pile on jobs to make ends meet, and could instead focus on building her deeply personal style. “I had been exposed to so many influences coming to New York. And maybe those first five years in Maine were a time when I was able to process them alone.”

Braunig knows her art history, and she gets why people connect her style back to Surrealism. But her artistic comrades are more like French self-portraitist Claude Cahun or even Frida Kahlo, she says, “who’s really making work that is influenced by events of her own life.” Her art might feel dreamy (or perhaps like an acid trip), but “it’s not a dream space. I really am thinking about actual forces in the actual world.”

For many years Braunig used her work to illustrate broader ideas around culture and gender, but her most recent output has taken a turn toward the autobiographical. A few years ago she went through a creative block that left her stranded from her art practice, unable to make anything. As she emerged from this period last year, she started making her most personal paintings yet.

That work went into her radiant show “Cauldron” at Magenta Plains this past spring, its title a reference to the messy, transformative stew of midlife. Its anchor painting was My Death (2025), which, along with several others from the show, will travel to Portland for “Wrist Painter.”

Sascha Braunig, My Death, 2025, oil on linen over panel.

She calls My Death an “explicit self-portrait” about her own reanimation—an emergence through the void of creative block. It’s juicy and haunting and beautiful. She employed those tentacles again, but this time they are like a life force attached to a sleeping Braunig. A mysterious figure made from strips of vermilion orange pulls on them like marionette strings, bringing the artist back to life.

“I’ve come to understand that painting is a survival practice,” she says. “That can be a framing practice where you’re using it to talk about ideas in the world, or it can be a practice where you’re directly processing your own experiences. Maybe those two things aren’t that different.”

For the PMA show, Braunig has made one brand-new work, titled Torsion. At 78 inches tall by 60 inches wide, it is the largest painting of her career. She previously used the title for a museum show in Norway, but realized she’d never given it to an individual painting. The term, in physics, refers to the twisting of an object around an axis: a towel wrung out to dry.

This new Torsion is a painting of a fleshy pink figure with a tiny, twisted torso standing on tiptoes within a gray box. The figure’s muscular back is to the viewer, its knees fanning out in a plié. Dramatic tentacles in orange and yellow trace its exaggerated outline.

The figure’s head is cut off by the box’s ceiling, and its arms stop around the elbows, where they encounter the side walls. Maybe the figure’s been blocked, held back by the confines of this shadowy encasement. Or maybe, somehow, it has broken free.

“Sascha Braunig: Wrist Painter” is on view at the Portland Art Museum in Maine from November 13, 2026, through March 14, 2027.

Sources at LAX tell TMZ that passengers waiting at the airport were told to use the bathroom and get food ... because the airport was preparing for an influx of landing flights.

TMZ obtained video from LAX on Friday showing passengers standing in long restroom lines ... as travelers scramble to take care of business before a flood of arriving passengers turns the toilets into a total traffic jam.

And with planes stuck on the tarmac amid the nationwide shutdown, things could really start to stink for arriving passengers ... 'cause once those floodgates open, the s***'s really gonna hit the fan!

The FAA also said inbound international flights would be handled on a case-by-case basis ... leaving travelers across the country dealing with major uncertainty.

HBO’s 5-Part Crime Drama Has One of the Best Seasons in TV History

📅 Nov 14, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

The Wire’s fourth season provided a far deeper look at Baltimore’s problems

The Wire’s fourth season provided a far deeper look at Baltimore’s problems

At the turn of the 21st century, HBO was the place for prestige crime drama. The launch of The Sopranos in 1999 paved the way for the 2002 premiere of The Wire, which focused on drug dealers in Baltimore and the police officers trying to disrupt their operations. The series is still one of the best shows of all time, and provided breakout roles for huge stars like Idris Elba and Michael B. Jordan. But while it was compelling from the beginning, The Wire truly achieved perfection in season 4 when the scope expanded beyond the drug trade to examine the cycle of institutional failure.

Season 1 of The Wire was tightly focused on the drug empire of Avon Barksdale (Wood Harris) and his business-savvy second-in-command Stringer Bell (Elba). Showrunner David Simon quickly established a brilliant mix of humor and pathos. The show could find comedy in something as simple as cops struggling to get new furniture into their office, and then gut audiences with the death of a relatable drug dealer. Season 2 shifted the focus to the city’s port system, a jarring change as many characters faded into the background to make room for the tragic tale of the Sobotka family. Season 3 was the most tightly linked to the show’s first season, largely completing the story of the Barksdale gang.

That made season 4 perfect for a soft reboot, a shift that was defined by star Dominic West all but disappearing after his relentless detective, Jimmy McNulty, was demoted for pissing off his bosses too many times. The detectives he previously worked with in the major crimes unit continue their work, investigating a vicious new gang that’s filling the void Barksdale left behind. Lester Freamon (Clarke Peters) effectively steps into the McNulty role of pursuing the truth, even when his supervisors would love for him to be a little less dogged, bringing a new methodical nature to the investigations instead of McNulty’s brash confidence. But while the cops vs. criminals beats are familiar, they become just one part of a far more ambitious season that fully capitalized on Simon’s knowledge of the city. (Simon worked as a journalist at The Baltimore Sun for 12 years before creating The Wire.)

The season’s largest secondary plot involves the students at a Baltimore middle school where former cop Roland Pryzbylewski (Jim True-Frost) begins teaching. The young actors do a phenomenal job establishing likable characters in terrible circumstances. You want to root for all of them, from the ambitious Randy Wagstaff (Maestro Harrell) to the fiercely protective Michael Lee (Tristan Wilds). By the end of season 4, the system has profoundly failed almost all of these kids, driving them to lives of addiction and violence. The Wire was always good at making most of its characters sympathetic, but seeing children trapped in such a brutal cycle really drives home the show’s themes of how crime thrives when institutions fail.

That theme also plays out in the mayoral campaign of Tommy Carcetti (Aidan Gillen). Carcetti was introduced in season 3 as an ambitious city council member, but he really shines in season 4 as he runs on reforming the Baltimore Police Department — and then quickly breaks his promises. Gillen’s oily performance as a political survivor who would doom his city to satisfy his own ambitions was what landed him the role of Littlefinger in Game of Thrones. Carcetti is even more compelling a character because he feels like a man who could have done good if he’d been willing to make sacrifices rather than feed his own ego.

The Wire season 4 is able to build so many new arcs because of the strong foundation established in the previous three seasons. The show is dense with rich characters who have grown significantly over the years even as they continue to struggle with many of the same problems. The cops and drug dealers alike feel world-weary as they deal with a more violent new generation represented by Marlo Stanfield (Jamie Hector) and his vicious enforcers Snoop (Felicia Pearson) and Chris Partlow (Gbenga Akinnagbe). Combined with the dark fate of most of the kids and Carcetti’s broken promises, the season creates the bleak feeling that everyone is trapped in a cycle of misery and decay.

Season 5 gave most of The Wire’s characters satisfying ends to their arcs and delivered a scathing critique of the downfall of American newspapers, commentary that has only become more profound 20 years later. But overall, it’s a big step down from season 4, spending too much time on a goofy fake serial killer plot and not enough on the nuanced character-driven stories that made season 4 feel so authentic. There are no bad seasons of The Wire, but season 4 perfectly achieved the show’s goals. It’s an alternately funny, tragic, and profound look at the complex ways that people are betrayed by systems that never really give them a chance to succeed.

SpaceX’s Starship Just Did Something for Only the 2nd Time. Saturday Could Be Even Bigger.

📅 Nov 14, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Starship reached orbit with 26 Starlink satellites on Flight 15, but the booster was diverted into the water and burst into flames.

Space Exploration Technologies Corp.'s (SPCX -0.59%) Starship Flight 15 lifted off from Starbase, Texas, on Friday, Nov. 13, at 5:47 p.m. ET in the company's attempt to pull off a "double-catch" of both stages of its Starship rocket for the first time.

But after a clean separation in the upper atmosphere, the lower half -- a Super Heavy booster -- was diverted off the coast, splashing down in the Gulf of Mexico, after which it burst into flames. The company has not shared details of the issue encountered.

While that kept the mission from achieving complete success, Starship — the upper stage — successfully reached orbit at 6:13 p.m. ET. That's only the second time the company's pulled that off, with SpaceX's Dan Huot calling it "a really great moment" on Flight 15's livestream.

The payload of 26 of Starlink's latest satellites is currently being deployed.

If the rest of the mission goes according to plan, Starship will reenter the atmosphere tomorrow with a catch attempt scheduled for 4:17 p.m. ET. SpaceX said before launch that it would attempt a catch only if all systems needed to guide it through the atmosphere and into the tower successfully were healthy. So, successfully reaching orbit doesn't guarantee that the catch will go ahead.

Even without a first catch, a successful second catch of Starship would be huge for the company, representing a major step toward making SpaceX's goal of a fully and rapidly reusable, heavy lift rocket a reality. The program is a major part of SpaceX's growth thesis.

In the third quarter, SpaceX brought in $14.6 billion in revenue, and the Space segment accounted for just $1.8 billion of that. Starship isn't really about the company's top line -- for the moment, that is.

In the near term, an operational Starship program's main job would be to get as many Starlink satellites into orbit as quickly and cheaply as possible. Long term, however, once the program scales and the bulk of the Starlink satellite network has been filled out, Starship would allow the company to greatly expand its launch service business, and the top-line will undoubtedly grow significantly.

The company's current workhorse, Falcon 9, reuses its booster but throws away its upper stage on every flight. The ability to reuse such a large rocket would drive down the cost of getting payloads of all sizes into space, helping it win larger contracts from enterprise and government customers alike.

SpaceX is aiming for full Starship capability around the end of 2027, but the program has seen significant setbacks and delays in its development. A successful catch tomorrow would go a long way in reassuring investors worried about further delays.

Getting Starship into orbit with a full load of Starlink satellites is a good day for SpaceX, but having the booster diverted into the water and burst into flames is a reminder that the hard part of this program -- the real essence of what makes it special -- is not getting to space, but getting back to Earth in one piece, over and over. That is what will change the economics of space.

Trump doesn’t plan to raise Nvidia, spy issue during China trip: U.S. officials

📅 2026-11-14  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

President t Donald Trump is not expected to raise allegations of a Chinese intelligence operation involving former Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang during his upcoming meeting with Chinese President Xi Jinping, senior U.S. officials said Friday.

Officials also said that Elon Musk, the CEO of Tesla and SpaceX, will accompany Trump on the trip to Shenzhen, China, for the Asia-Pacific Economic Cooperation summit on Nov. 18 and 19.

The White House, earlier Friday, said Trump plans to have a three-way summit there with Xi and Russian President Vladimir Putin.

Jensen Huang's relationship with the suspected spy, Alice Huang, who is not related to him, has raised concerns that a Singapore-based company where she was an executive diverted restricted Nvidia chips to China.

"The situation with Nvidia should not really have any bearing on the trip," an official said on the press call, speaking on the condition that they not be identified.

"In all of our discussions and negotiations with the Chinese, we do not negotiate American export controls ... and we don't expect it to figure at all in any of our conversations with the Chinese while we're there."

Asked if Trump would raise the allegations against Alice Huang to Chinese leader Xi Jinping, the official said, "The focus of a meeting with President Xi, at least on the economic side, is going to be about implementation of the trade agreement."

"When it comes to some of these national security matters and examinations and investigations that are going on in the U.S., [that is] probably not something that we're going to read the Chinese in on at this moment," the official said.

Another official said, "I don't expect the president to raise it — the Alice Huang issue."

China has called the allegations against Alice Huang baseless.

Huang resigned this week as Nvidia's CEO after damning reports that he had an affair with Alice Huang, who was an executive of Megaspeed, a cloud-computing company that is being eyed for possible facilitation of sending billions of dollars worth of Nvidia chips in violation of U.S. restrictions on those chips.

The scandal has rocked Nvidia and raised questions about whether the Commerce Department, which is led by Howard Lutnick, has been aggressive enough at blocking advanced chips from ending up in China.

During Friday's call, a reporter asked why Musk was going on the trip with Trump.

"I don't want to speak for the president on exactly why Elon's going," an official said.

"Obviously, he has tremendous business interests in the U.S.-China relationship. He's a big part of our national security," the official said. "There are numerous reasons, given the engagement that the president is going to have, where I think Elon would be of tremendous benefit, just even as an advisor to the president."

FAA grounds departing flights nationwide, citing air traffic communication system outage

📅 2026-11-13  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

The Federal Aviation Administration halted thousands of departing flights nationwide on Friday afternoon, a rare measure, blaming communications issues that affected "multiple" air traffic control centers. More than 10,000 flights were disrupted.

Homeland Security Secretary Markwayne Mullin said Friday at the White House that there was no indication that the issues were a threat to the United States.

The FAA said in post on X that there was a "temporary telecommunications outage" and that it halted flights out of an "abundance of caution." The issues were related to communications and radar problems, according to an internal message from Alaska Airlines, which was seen by CNBC.

Airlines said en route flights would continue, but carriers were preparing to cancel flights as the disruption wore on. JetBlue Airways told staff it would cancel the remainder of the day's flights, aside from European routes, as well as others scheduled for Saturday so that crews and planes are in place to resume operations, according to a memo seen by CNBC.

FAA administrator Bryan Bedford had a call with airline CEOs around 6 p.m. indicating that it was unclear when the situation would be resolved, according to people familiar with the matter.

As of 5:56 p.m. ET, some outbound international traffic was in the air, according to Flightradar24, flight-tracking site. That included a Lufthansa flight from Boston to Frankfurt, Germany, that departed at 5:21 p.m. ET and an ITA Airways flight from Boston to Rome that left at 5:24 p.m. ET, according to the site.

As of 6:50 p.m. ET, cargo and passenger flights were also departing the West Coast for Asia and Hawaii.

Flights will be allowed to depart as en route traffic facilities recover, "through coordination with the air traffic control system," the FAA said at about 6 p.m..

Airlines said flights in the air would continue.

When it issued the ground stop at about 4 p.m., the FAA said the communications issues were "impacting multiple Air Route Traffic Control Centers." These facilities guide airplanes at high altitudes along their routes.

The FAA's technicians are investigating, the agency said.

A ground stop halts flights at their departure point. Disruptions tend to snowball if ground stops drag on as flight crews reach their maximum number of duty hours for a day.

There were 11,000 flights scheduled to depart U.S. airports after 4 p.m. ET on Friday, according to flight-data firm Cirium. There were about 600 diverted flights on Friday, 104 of them sent to other airports, Cirium said.

Nationwide ground stops are extremely rare.

In January 2023, the FAA halted flights because of issues with a pilot alert system. Before that, traffic was halted nationwide after the Sept. 11, 2001, terror attacks.

Technical issues have repeatedly snarled air traffic in the U.S. in recent years.

In September, hundreds of flights into Newark, New Jersey, and Philadelphia were canceled, delayed or diverted after a Verizon fiber cable was accidentally cut. Officials said it occurred during construction in New Jersey, severing a backup feed of key data for air traffic controllers.

In 2025, air traffic controllers lost contact with aircraft heading to and from Newark Liberty International Airport, an equipment failure that led to massive flight delays and raised more concerns about aging U.S. aviation infrastructure and staffing shortages.

It wasn't immediately clear what caused Friday's problem.

Inbound international flights will be managed on a case by case basis, the FAA said.

The major U.S. airlines issued statements saying they would work with customers who were affected by the ground stop and encouraged travelers to check for updates.

— CNBC's Kevin Breuninger and Phil LeBeau contributed to this article.
